## Resolved Plot Threads
- The escape from Velkynvelve slave pen was completed when prisoners overcame guard tower defenders and secured weapons and armor, marking the first phase of their breakout `[ch 003 / 003.01; ch 003 / 003.03]`.
- Coordinate escape using rapport spores succeeded as Stool’s telepathic spores enabled silent communication and synchronized action; the effect expired after one hour `[ch 003 / 003.01; ch 003 / spells]`.
- The guard tower assault was won after the party defeated drow guards and Elite Warrior Imbros, securing weapons, armor, rope, and caltrops `[ch 003 / 003.02; ch 003 / 003.03]`.
- Armory equipment was secured with short swords, hand crossbows, studded leather armor, rope, and caltrops retrieved from the tower’s upper chamber `[ch 003 / 003.04]`.
- Shuushar’s role as guide ended when he departed to lead kuo-toa refugees toward a new society, closing his direct involvement with the party `[ch 009 / 009.06; ch 010 / 010.01]`.
- Buppido’s fate was resolved when he was revealed as a mad cultist who killed Yuk Yuk, erected a shrine, attacked the party, and was slain by Magic Missile `[ch 015 / 015.06; ch 015 / 015.07]`.
- The NDA negotiation with Werz was completed when Werz paid 1,100 gp and agreed to confidentiality, ending the immediate financial negotiation `[ch 018 / 018.05]`.
- The red dragon egg was successfully delivered to Gartokkar, who confirmed its purpose was to replace Themberchaud, resolving the mission objective `[ch 026 / 026.06]`.
- The Lost Tomb of Khaem was fully explored and looted; its main chamber and decoy were cleared of threats, with no further dangers remaining `[ch 029 / 029.01; ch 029 / 029.03]`.
- Brysis’s imprisonment ended when she was destroyed after a millennium of confinement, freeing the sentient sword Dawnbringer `[ch 029 / 029.01]`.
- Eldeth’s trail-obscuring effort succeeded in breaking the drow pursuit, reducing it to zero for the time being `[ch 029 / 029.04]`.
- Zalthir’s spore-induced madness was cured by Thorin’s Lesser Restoration; he apologized and admitted the compulsion was inexplicable, ending the immediate threat to party cohesion `[ch 032 / 032.02]`.
- Daz’s hallucinations from spores subsided after the vision ended and the party retreated; he regained full awareness with no further effects noted `[ch 032 / 032.02]`.
- The fate of Sarith’s infection was resolved when his body exploded and became a drow spore servant, confirming the terminal progression of the corruption he carried since earlier encounters `[ch 031 / 031.04; ch 031 / Memorable Moments]`.
- The “tears” bet was settled by Thorin’s promise of ten slimy silver pieces to Jimjar after surviving the flood ordeal `[ch 034 / 034.08]`.
- The Pudding King’s threat was ended when he dissolved after being defeated, eliminating his telepathic declaration and immediate menace `[ch 047 / 047.08]`.
- The Pudding King’s army command was broken when he died, and his allies were defeated, ending his control over the ooze horde `[ch 047 / 047.09]`.
- The ooze crisis in Traders’ Grotto was resolved as the uncontrolled ooze swarm pacified and dispersed, judged clear within days to a week `[ch 048 / 048.05]`.
- The psychic static in the Hall of Melting Mirrors was silenced after the harmonic center was located `[ch 048 / 048.02]`.
- Structural integrity of support beams was secured, miners were healed, and Thorin was honored, resolving the immediate collapse threat `[ch 048 / 048.03]`.
- Blingdenstone Council negotiations were resolved through compromise, establishing a new political balance among miners, wererats, ghosts, and trade interests `[ch 048 / 048.06]`.
- Ilvara Mizzrym was killed when Gyrgum’s Guiding Bolt triggered her radiant vulnerability, causing her to explode into poisonous spores `[ch 051 / 051.06]`.
- The Heart Fungus was destroyed by Thorin’s Dawnbringer strikes, ending its threat and causing Gyrgum to drop his protective sphere `[ch 051 / 051.02]`.
- The House T'sarran Assault Team was eliminated: three guards incinerated by Fireball, the mage killed by Zalthir, and the elite warrior retreated with a threat `[ch 051 / 051.04; ch 051 / 051.06]`.
- Asha Vandree’s delusion ended with her death; her theological writings were recovered as evidence of her corruption `[ch 053 / 053.02; ch 053 / 053.03]`.
- Ilvara’s pursuit was resolved when her force was destroyed, her body looted, and her correspondence ceased, making her disappearance known to House T'sarran `[ch 053 / 053.03; ch 053 / 053.04]`.
- The Fungal Altar threat was neutralized: the heart fungus was harvested, its spore remnant burned, and the chamber cleared of corruption `[ch 053 / 053.04]`.
- Mercenary escort was fulfilled when Nym and Kaelira departed after escorting Daz to the surface `[ch 053 / 053.05]`.
- The decoy key was confirmed as non-magical and retained as a potential tool for deception `[ch 063 / 063.01]`.
- The first Manshoon simulacrum was destroyed by Zalthir, reverting to snow and melting in the magma chamber `[ch 069 / 069.02]`.
- The final door’s riddle was solved when Gyrgum spoke “a candle” with genuine intent, fulfilling the enchantment’s requirement `[ch 070 / 070.03]`.
- Manshoon’s simulacrum was confirmed dead after being thrown into the lava pit and will not return `[ch 070 / 070.02]`.
- Edvaldo’s immediate threat ended when the doppelgänger fled toward the anti-magic barrier and was no longer present in the chamber `[ch 070 / 070.03]`.
- The explosive fail-safe was neutralized because the simulacrum was removed from the warded chamber before disposal, triggering no explosion `[ch 070 / 070.02]`.

## Active Quests & Open Threads
- **Daz’s hidden origins and lineage** — Daz suspects his true name is “Daz’issin,” a softened form used by mothers hoping their sons would survive, and that his lineage was deliberately erased; House Baenre in Menzoberranzan is suspected of funding his pursuers for eleven years [ch 056 / 056.07; ch 067 / 067.02].
- **The Book of Vile Darkness** — Daz has begun reading the sentient artifact, receiving information about the Underdark from an untrustworthy source; its corruption remains active, and the surviving Avowed Readers will not permit the party to leave with it [ch 070 / 070.04; ch 070 / items].
- **The four surviving Alaundo prophecy crystals** — Gyrgum secured them after Edvaldo destroyed two; their unrevealed prophecies are likely tied to the party, including one foretelling Eldeth’s return to a hall of stone bearing demon names and Thorin’s summoning to an ancient forge [ch 070 / 070.03; ch 070 / items].
- **The metal-hand courier of Manshoon** — A'lai confirmed Manshoon’s agent delivers book lists with a glove concealing a metal hand; the courier’s identity and connection to the broader conspiracy remain unknown [ch 064 / 064.04].
- **The obsidian door’s final guardian and mechanism** — The door opened after Gyrgum spoke “a candle,” but the chamber above contains the means to destroy the Vault’s collection, which the party has not yet located [ch 068 / 068.05; ch 069 / 069.05].
- **The second Manshoon simulacrum’s bargain** — A second, cheaper-robed simulacrum offered ten prismatic gemstones and one book in exchange for opening the Obsidian Door; the gemstones remain in his possession and their prophecies are unexplored [ch 069 / 069.05].
- **The “over 500 feet” tower claim** — Edvaldo asserted Candlekeep’s High Tower is “over 500 feet” tall without verification; whether this is accurate or a deception remains unresolved [ch 068 / 068.03; ch 068 / npcs].
- **The “factory” of Manshoon simulacra** — Gyrgum observed that multiple duplicates must be produced, implying an active, hidden mechanism for creating them; the location and method of production are unknown [ch 069 / 069.04].
- **The real Manshoon’s vengeance** — The real Manshoon’s final words promised eternal vengeance after his simulacrum was destroyed; the party now faces the wrath of the original, unbound archlich [ch 070 / 070.02; ch 070 / npcs].
- **The “dancing myconid parade” and fungal wedding** — The party observed a procession of myconids announcing a wedding celebration, strongly tied to Zuggtmoy’s influence; the nature, location, and participants of this fungal wedding remain unknown [ch 040 / 040.02; ch 035 / 035.04].
- **The fungal wedding ingredients for acid-resistant gear** — Jimjar’s bet requires the party to recover ingredients from the fungal wedding celebration to craft acid-resistant gear; the location and method of acquisition are unresolved [ch 035 / 035.04].
- **The identity of the “bride” (Zuggtmoy)** — Ilvara’s devotion to Zuggtmoy as “the bride” and the fungal corruption spreading through drow and myconids confirm her active influence; the party has not yet acted to stop her encroachment [ch 049 / 049.03; ch 048 / 048.07].
- **Glabbagool’s evolution and sentience** — Glabbagool now occupies the Pudding King’s body, demonstrates control over viscosity, and is being considered for monk training; his potential to become a T-1000-style entity and his role as a mobile waste processor remain open-ended [ch 047 / 047.10; ch 048 / 048.07].
- **The Bone Die** — A 20-sided die carved from a saint’s knucklebone, won in Gracklstugh, pacified an ooze army once and is now inert; its origin and potential future use are unknown [ch 048 / 048.05].
- **The Voucher of the Varmint** — A braided rat’s foot gifted to Daz by Chipgrin, recognized by wererats and outcasts from Blingdenstone to Menzoberranzan; its diplomatic limits and full utility are untested [ch 048 / 048.07].
- **The six-pointed star symbol** — Elin draws this shape repeatedly; it matches charcoal rubbings from Daggerford and the Black-Banner Five marker from the Underdark; its meaning and origin are unknown [ch 056 / 056.02; ch 056 / 056.07].
- **The spreading surface madness** — Fractured speech patterns in Eldred, the Field Ward preacher, and the Beregost scholar, along with the syncopated chant heard on the wind near Candlekeep, confirm the Underdark’s corruption is bleeding into the surface world [ch 055 / 055.04; ch 056 / 056.03].
- **The Endless Chant** — The same syncopated chant pattern appears in multiple surface locations; its ritualistic nature and source are unknown, but it is linked to Zuggtmoy’s influence [ch 056 / 056.03; ch 056 / 056.07].
- **The sealed records of Candlekeep** — Sister Yvenne confirmed the public record is sealed on one house; Daz realizes Candlekeep itself is complicit in erasing knowledge, including the erased fourth seat of Menzoberranzan [ch 056 / 056.07].
- **The Ardragon** — A black-hooded Kenku hovering a foot off the ground, mimicking a hawk, leaving coins uncollected on caravan trails north of Yartar; its connection to Whistler and purpose are unknown [ch 055 / 055.04].
- **Kestler’s Temple Fire** — A southern temple’s fire still burns; its keeper has not written in five years and has not been seen in three; Gyrgum calls it an omen, but its significance is unexplained [ch 055 / 055.04].
- **Eldred’s fractured voice** — A courier speaks in two voices — his own and a younger version — identified as the same madness from the Underdark; the cause and spread of this condition are unresolved [ch 055 / 055.04].
- **Mortal Agency Doctrine** — Gyrgum received the pamphlet *Stroud and the Human Agency Hypothesis*, introducing a philosophical faction rejecting divine influence; its impact on the party’s divine ties is unexplored [ch 055 / 055.03].
- **Glabbagool’s cheese curiosity** — Glabbagool asked if dairy is bad for people, marking a new dimension of his sentience and curiosity; his dietary preferences and social integration remain open [ch 055 / 055.04].
- **Dawnbringer’s therapeutic journey** — Thorin seeks a “Sword Whisperer” at Candlekeep to help Dawnbringer process trauma; she rejects therapy but allows spending, and her silence after being asked about her wielder indicates unresolved trauma [ch 053 / 053.06; ch 056 / 056.06].
- **The Bone King’s song** — A cryptic note on Khell-Vire’s desk reads: “Zalthir, the Underdark, until the Bone King sings”; neither Zalthir nor Khell-Vire understand its meaning, but it is tied to Zalthir’s trial and unresolved past [ch 056 / 056.05].
- **The drow captive in the bag of holding** — Polly Pocket, a shrunken drow spy, is guarded by Glabbagool and believes she can escape; her fate and potential as a messenger remain live [ch 054 / 054.05; ch 054 / 054.06].
- **Vareth’s research on orc Bahamut converts** — Vareth has secured Gyrgum as a “living data point”; his research has become prominent due to the Stroud-Protanther events, and Khell-Vire specifically wishes to study Gyrgum [ch 054 / 054.06].
- **Daz’s contemplation of a counter-account** — Daz privately resolves to write his own version of the War of the Dragons to obscure truths and preserve the real story’s magnitude; this may influence future narratives [ch 054 / 054.06].
- **Glabbagool’s question to Candlekeep scholars** — The party secured Glabbagool his own question to the scholars; what he asks and what he learns is now an open thread [ch 054 / 054.05].
- **The First Reader’s collection of drow prison journals** — The First Reader’s excitement over the Velkynvelve Prisoner Journal confirms it is a recognized genre; deeper institutional knowledge of drow captivity is implied [ch 054 / 054.06].
- **House T’sarran’s surveillance of Daz** — A drow spy confirms House T’sarran is using Daz as bait to identify his unknown protector; the party now knows they are being watched for this purpose [ch 054 / 054.04].
- **The “struck” fourth seat of Menzoberranzan** — Daz discovered the term “struck” refers to the extinction of a drow house, implying political censorship; the ritual archive privileges of the fourth seat make its erasure significant [ch 056 / 056.07].
- **The “dustsight spectacles’ darkness vision”** — The GM ruled the spectacles can see through magical darkness, an ability not in the rules as written; this creates a new tactical variable [ch 045 / 045.03].
- **The “Ember Grapple” identity** — The party adopted this name based on Zalthir’s Street Justice and Daz’s Cloud of Daggers synergy; its use as a public moniker is now established [ch 052 / 052.03].
- **The “Gyrgumite School” of philosophy** — Gyrgum introduced himself as its founder to the Council of Twelve, derailing a murder investigation; its acceptance and influence are now active [ch 057 / 057.05].
- **The Murder of Janussi** — The Keeper of Tomes was found dead with his heart removed and ribs shattered; the party has multiple suspects (Bookwyrm, Daral, Sylvira, Alkrist, A'lai) and unresolved questions about the stolen locket, sapphire, and *The Golden Ass* [ch 057 / 057.07; ch 061 / 061.07].
- **The missing sapphire and locket** — Though Alkrist confessed to poisoning Janussi, the thefts of the locket (containing one High Tower key) and the sapphire (used in Drawmij’s Instant Summons) remain unexplained and tied to the heart removal [ch 061 / 061.07].
- **Bookwyrm’s complicity** — Bookwyrm concealed Alkrist’s confession and may have impersonated Sylvira using polymorph magic; she is a green dragonborn (Skoda Vanaster) and her motives remain unexplored [ch 061 / 061.07; ch 062 / npcs].
- **The High Tower key deception** — Kalan gave the party a decoy key; the real key #2 is with Gyrgum, key #1 is held by A'lai, and the sapphire is in Gyrgum’s possession; the door remains sealed [ch 063 / 063.01; ch 064 / 064.04].
- **The Orrery of the Astronomicon surveillance** — Sylvira observed three non-staff individuals inside the Orrery; Manshoon’s forces or other enemies are confirmed infiltrating Candlekeep’s inner sanctum [ch 067 / 067.03].
- **The Vault of Secrets’ final guardian and obsidian door** — The party has decoded six of seven instructions and located three components; the final guardian and obsidian door remain unencountered [ch 067 / 067.03].
- **Thorin’s fall into the well** — Thorin plunged into the dark circular well below the collapsing staircase; his survival and location are unknown [ch 067 / 067.06].
- **The “factory” of Manshoon simulacra** — Gyrgum observed, “There must be a whole factory of these things”; the location and method of production remain unknown [ch 069 / 069.04].
- **The “prismatic gemstones”** — Ten gemstones, four dark, containing prophecies, are held by the second Manshoon simulacrum and offered as part of a bargain; their contents and significance are unexplored [ch 069 / 069.05].
- **The “prophecy of the dwarf and unlit forge”** — A disembodied voice prophesies Eldeth returning to the Hall of Stone with demon names in her mouth, alongside Thorin and Zalthir; its fulfillment is pending [ch 069 / 069.04].

## Party Current Situation
- The party is in the Inner Book Chamber of Candlekeep’s Obsidian Tower, having just opened the sealed door by speaking “a candle” in darkness, spoken with genuine intent by Gyrgum [ch 070 / 070.03].
- Group name is Ember Grapple, formally adopted in the prior session [ch 064 / 064.01].
- Level is 9th [ch 065 / end].
- Daz has donned the Robe of the Archmagi (unattuned, worn for style) and is currently manipulating the Book of Vile Darkness with Mage Hand; he has chosen to seek knowledge of how to defeat the demons over self-knowledge; the book’s revelations about Underdark events are underway but its narrator is untrustworthy; he has not been corrupted but remains under its influence [ch 070 / 070.04].
- Gyrgum has secured the four remaining prophecy crystals; he has cast Guiding Bolt as a readied action against Edvaldo; he is the verified Reader who opened the door; he holds the real High Tower key and the stolen sapphire [ch 070 / 070.03; ch 059 / 059.03; ch 065 / 065.03].
- Zalthir has used Shadow Step, Stunning Strike, and his Eldritch Claw Tattoo to grapple and throw both Manshoon simulacra into the lava pit; he has dragged Edvaldo away from the chamber and is currently restraining him; he has confirmed his shadow monk training with Glabbagool as his sidekick [ch 070 / 070.02; ch 059 / 059.06].
- Thorin wields Dawnbringer (a sentient +2 radiant weapon), used Menacing Attack to frighten Edvaldo, and stands between the doppelganger and the prophecy crystals; he is emotionally bonded to the sword and has accepted its guidance [ch 070 / 070.03; ch 055 / 055.05].
- Glabbagool, now an eighth-level sidekick to Zalthir with Shadow Step, Flurry of Blows, Acidic Shadow-Ki, and Corrosive Embrace, holds one real High Tower key inside his body; he is visibly taller and less “pancakey” [ch 059 / 059.06; ch 065 / 065.03].
- Edvaldo, a doppelganger and former Avowed Reader, has been subdued and dragged away by Zalthir; he attempted to destroy the prophecy crystals and escape after Manshoon’s death; he is currently restrained and under threat of Guiding Bolt [ch 070 / 070.03].
- The party has destroyed two of Alaundo’s prophecy crystals; the remaining four are secured; the Book of Vile Darkness is in their possession and actively communicating with Daz [ch 070 / 070.03; ch 070 / 070.04].
- The surviving Avowed Readers are expected to arrive within minutes, and the party knows they will not be allowed to leave with the Book of Vile Darkness or the prophecy crystals without confrontation [ch 070 / 070.04].
- Next intention: Survive the imminent arrival of Candlekeep’s Avowed Readers; decide whether to flee, negotiate, or fight to retain the Book of Vile Darkness and the prophecy crystals; determine the true nature of the book’s revelations and whether to act on them; resolve Edvaldo’s fate and the location of the second High Tower key [ch 070 / 070.04].
